## 0.1.29

### Patch Changes

- 362ed4e: A relation's caption stands over its own run of the band again. Measuring a chip of the band as the chip, so a tie ends on it, also moved the captions: a chip sits lower in its slot than a card of several in the same row, so the two read as different rows and "their songs" stood over the "+more" card at the far right while "the songs they produced" hung inside its chip's slot. Captions are placed by the slot the layout made each row of, and ties still end on the chip; `verify scale` holds `theBandReads` again.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 7966f5d: A change says what it was based on, and a link made less than sure is drawn as such until a person keeps it (FR-172). An author said who moved and nothing said why one change was made or how sure its maker was, so a background actor that links and prunes on its own ("Graview, tidying") could not show its reasons or be told apart from a person's work. An op may now carry `lineage: { basis, confidence?, model? }` — given as `ApplyOptions.lineage` to `apply`, `applyAll`, `applyPrimitives` and `undo`, or on an `AppendOp` — checked before anything is kept (a basis of 1–500 characters, a confidence from 0 to 1, a model of 1–120) and kept in the log, on the batch and on the wire. On the op rather than its author, because an author is the same for every change a seat makes and a basis is this change's own. It is said where the change is: "based on “both name 12 Harbor Street” · 60% sure · jev-1" on a record page's history line and in the scene's recent changes, and a record page says what the record was made on. A link whose making op was less than sure, by anyone but a person, is provisional: the scene draws it in short dashes and quieter, its basis on hover and in its acts when it is pressed or long-pressed, and a record page marks it "unsure", the mark saying the basis and offering "Keep this link". Keeping it is `keep-link` (`KEEP_LINK`), an act every store answers to and no list offers, which takes the link away and puts it back as the person's op; any person's op that takes the link away does too, and undoing the keep makes it provisional again. An undo of a change with a lineage keeps it as `undoneLineage`, so a host can read "do not make this again" — a merge (FR-173) as much as a link. `@graview/core/provenance` is the one answer every face and host reads — `linkProvenance(store, edge)`, `isProvisional`, `provisionalLinks`, `provenanceWords` — fetched with what draws a link, so the hosted page's first load stays under its budget. Nothing in the framework decides what to link.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `lineage` and `undoneLineage`, additive within op format 1: an op without them reads and folds as before, and a build that predates them keeps them on an op it receives. `Batch` gains an optional `lineage`. The wire's `call` and `undo`, on the socket and over `POST /graview/ops`, may carry `lineage`; a change whose lineage is not one is refused as `invalid`, and a server that predates it ignores the field. Every store answers to `keep-link` (`{ kind, from, to }`), which no list of acts — `allMutations`, `permittedMutations`, an agent's tools — offers; an app that declares its own `keep-link` keeps it. Stored formats, the document format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-172. What a host does: a background actor passes `lineage` with each change it makes, reads `linkProvenance` or `provisionalLinks` for what a person has not yet kept, and reads an undo's `undoneLineage` for what a person declined.
- 6764d85: A district's "⚠ 3" on the map is a control. It was dead text: the name card said a district had problems and nothing on the map said which. It is a button now, a fingertip tall, named for what it opens ("3 problems in Tasks"), and its hover says the first problem in the rule's own sentence. Pressed, or Enter or Space on it, it chooses the district and brings its problems up at the head of the panel beside the map (the sheet on a phone): the bar's own rows, each in the words the problems list uses, with the number of ways to fix it, and a press that chooses the record it names and opens its acts — the repairs lead. The count is the bar's source, the broken rules that name one of the district's records, so the card, the panel and the bar never say two things; the records they name are outlined on the map while the panel is open, and the keyboard goes back to the count when the panel is put away, never to the page. A district's member count is a plain number everywhere — it says "36 rooms" on hover — and is never painted as the warning: the flat district cards on the ground and a group's header used to show "⚠ 36" on a district with one problem, because some member was flagged. They say "36" and, apart from it, the same "⚠ 1" button, which from the ground rises to the map with the problems open. A row's "+n" says what it is on hover. The panel's problems section has a proper heading with a visible focus, and a problem's sentence is said once, with how many ways to fix it beneath.
  
  A district's name is not moved by hand from altitude. The city is laid out by the data and a name stands under its own plot; a drag that starts on a name is the ground's own gesture, the one empty ground makes, and pans the city, plot and name together; it writes no pin. The name is still pressed to choose it and double-pressed to go in. A `pin.kind:<kind>` an old bookmark carries is ignored from altitude, and no plot wears the pinned curb for one. A record's card is still moved by hand, and a district's pin on the ground is as it was.
  
  The panel beside the map floats over it: a surface inset from the right edge, the top (under the bar) and the foot by the app's gap, with the app's radius on every corner, its own edge and the real elevation, rather than a column running flush to the picture's edges. What it holds is clipped to the radius. The city is still laid out in the room left of it — `--graview-dock` is now the panel's width and the gap on each side of it, and a phone's `--graview-sheet` its height, the gap and the safe-area inset — so no district, name or control stands under it.
  
  The scene is painted the whole picture, and the panel floats over it. The ground, grid, depth and tint run under the panel and show in the gap around it, rather than ending where the panel begins: the scene's box is no longer narrowed by `--graview-dock` or shortened by `--graview-sheet`. What the panel takes from the map is the city's fit and the camera's reach — `LayoutOptions.dock` fits the city in what is left, `Layout.visible` is the part the reader sees (the camera's bounds, the edge signs and the zoom's center are for it), and the zoom control, the edge signs and Up and the Key keep clear of the panel. Pointers over the gap belong to the scene; over the panel, to the panel.
  
  Compatibility: additive. A district card's problem count is a button where it was a span; nothing a host wrote reaches it. A district's pin is ignored from altitude, which moves a district a bookmark had placed back under its plot.
- a5c260f: A hosted page loads thirteen chunks before it draws where it loaded twenty-three, and 7.6 KB less (5.0 KB gzipped), so the round's money formats have room after its repeating dates and field-level sight. A bundler splits what a page loads first by which of its doors (each part fetched with `import()`) reach each file, so the frame's files were spread over twenty-three chunks, each with its own list of names in and out and each compressed alone. Every door in `@graview/core`, `@graview/react` and `@graview/primitives` now imports the barrel the page drew first (`@graview/core`, `@graview/react/provider`, `@graview/primitives/frame`) for nothing but that, which makes the files every door reaches one set and costs a door nothing at run time. What draws a notice is fetched with the first notice and prefetched once the page is idle (`notice-stacks.tsx`); the board and the two live regions that say each notice aloud stay up front, so a notice is said at once, and `fetchNotices()` fetches it at will (the embed's `preload` does). What only fetched faces say or do left the barrels a page imports: `edgeWords` and `valueWords` for `@graview/core/words`, and a face's moves (`withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin`, `edgeSelectionId`) for a new `@graview/layout/view-moves`. Built as Cloud builds it over the vendors document, the page that compiles its document goes from 549 727 bytes (192 368 gzipped) to 541 994 (187 254), and the page handed a compiled app from 507 528 (177 639) to 499 794 (172 530). Their budgets fall to keep about 8 KB of room (3.5 KB gzipped): 537.5 KB (186.4 gzipped) from 538.0 (188.0), and 496.0 KB (172.0 gzipped) from 497.5 (173.5). No face loses anything: the pages face alone is 3.4 KB less, and every face fetched together 4.4 KB less.
  
  Compatibility: BREAKING imports. `edgeWords` and `valueWords` move from `@graview/core` to `@graview/core/words`; `withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin` and `edgeSelectionId` move from `@graview/layout` and `@graview/layout/view` to `@graview/layout/view-moves`; `FOOT_MOVED`, `FOOT_OBSTACLES`, `NARROW_PICTURE`, `placeAtTheFoot` and `placeAtTheTop` leave `@graview/primitives/frame` (still on `@graview/primitives` and `@graview/primitives/pages`). `withoutMoves` stays on `@graview/layout/view`. New: `@graview/layout/view-moves`, and `fetchNotices` from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`. Ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: import those names from their new entries; its shell built from these sources loads about 7.5 KB less before the app draws.
- 3accacb: A kind opens to something useful. Gone into, a district the app drew no picture of — "open", a double press, `#focus=aggregate:<kind>` — drew six chips and "+30 more" in a box floating in an empty field, under a "⚠ 36" that was the number of rooms in the warning tone: nothing said what the records were, what was wrong with them or what to do. It opens now to the kind's stop, in the box a lens fills, derived from the declaration and the records. Its head says the plural, how many and what they are, and offers the kind's creating act first ("Add a room"), asked in place, with the rest behind "Acts…". Under it, only where there is something to say: how many are overdue or due in the next seven days on its due date, each a press that narrows the list; its problems counted by the rule that found them ("1 problem in rooms: A workshop fits its room"), each opening the bar's own rows and their repairs, and "Show only the rooms with a problem"; and what was added or changed since this reader last looked. Then the picture its data chooses: a board by its main choice (the lifecycle's field, or one called status, stage, state or phase) up to 300 records; a calendar on its main date where today's month holds records a fortnight long or less; otherwise a table of the kind's glance and its relations, read as the far records' names, an empty value said as a quiet dash ("none" to a screen reader), 50 rows at a time with "Show 50 more". Each row is its record to the scene, as a record is anywhere: pressed, it is chosen; right-clicked, long-pressed or given the menu key, its acts open; its name goes to it and its "⋯", drawn with an edge at rest, opens the same acts. Opened from altitude — a double press on its district, or Enter on it chosen — a kind with no picture opens to its stop in the panel beside the map a lens opens in, taking the place of a lens or another kind's stop already open there (one panel at a time; with a lens open it used to open behind it), its district shut on the map; the address says so, the keyboard lands on its heading, and Back returns to what was open. The other shape is one press away, the stop says the picture is derived from the declaration, and "Keep as a lens" keeps it as the seat keeps a view it drew (`add-lens`). The stop is a region named by its heading, a real table on a desk and rows of words in a narrow box, with 44-pixel targets under a finger and nothing scrolling sideways; it is fetched when a district is first gone into, so a page that never opens one carries a door of about a hundred bytes. `@graview/tools/draft` gains `recipeDraft(recipe, options)`, a draft from a recipe with no ask, titled and judged as an asked-for one ("Every room" for a plain list). The graview-lists skill says what a kind opens to and what to declare first, and `pnpm verify kind-stop` walks every example's kinds with no picture at a desk and a phone, light and dark.
  
  Compatibility: from altitude, opening a district with no picture now focuses it (`#overview=1&focus=aggregate:<kind>`) and draws its stop in `[data-testid="lens-panel"]`, where it used to choose it (`sel=kind:<kind>`, the district panel), which a single press still does; `LayoutOptions.besideTheMap` (set by the scene where a panel is drawn) keeps a focused group's district shut and stamps nothing in the city's middle. A view an app registers for a kind at many × full replaces the stop as before; the framework's default for that cell is now the stop, fetched lazily, drawing the old list until it arrives. New test ids are `kind-stop`, `kind-stop-primary`, `kind-stop-acts`, `kind-stop-problems`, `kind-stop-as-<shape>`, `kind-stop-keep`, `kind-stop-table`, `kind-stop-row-go`, `kind-stop-row-acts`, `kind-stop-more` and the arranging line `kind-arrange-*`, and the table's rows carry `data-graview-pick` as a record's mark does; `@graview/tools/draft` adds `recipeDraft`. The embed's every-face budget is raised by the stop's chunk (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kind-opens-to-something-useful-every-face.json`); the hosted page's up-front budget is unchanged.
- 3cd624d: A lens is listed once, however many kinds it draws. The program's scene bar read "The whole thing · What the talks are about · The timetable · The timetable · A dozen workshops": its one calendar over talks and workshops was registered on each kind, so that either district goes in to it, and every list of places took each registration for a picture of its own. A place now says when it is one picture over several kinds (`Place.over`, given by `ViewMeta.over`, which `registerDeclaredLenses` sets from the lens's bindings), and every list reads it through `onePerPicture`: the scene's bar and its tabs, the Pages bar and its More, the gallery and the pictures' page, Find and `search_graph`, the seat's places, `placesOf` and `placesFrom`, `graview describe`, and the slugs a guest view may link to. A district of either kind still goes in to it, the tab is pressed from either, and choosing it keeps the district in focus when it is one of its kinds. Its page is at `/places/<as>` alone and draws every kind it is over, with each kind's list beside it. Two different lenses that share a title on different kinds are another matter: `graview check` warns `lens-title-repeated`, and the bars and the gallery say whose — "The timetable · talks" (`placeLabel`).
  
  Compatibility: `placesOf` and `placesFrom` list a picture over several kinds once, under the first of its kinds in the arrangement's order, with a new optional `over` naming every kind it is over; its `address` is `/places/<as>` and its `stop` `#view=<as>`, where before each kind had an entry at `/places/<as>?of=<plural>` (those addresses still open the picture). `describe_place` takes the one slug as before. A search's place hit for such a picture comes once. `Place`, `ViewMeta` and `ViewRegistration` gain an optional `over`; `onePerPicture`, `samePicture` and `placeLabel` are new exports of `@graview/core`, and `sharesItsName` no longer counts a picture's own other kinds. Check gains the warning `lens-title-repeated`. Stored formats, the document format and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
  
  The embed's budgets are raised by what this adds (`scripts/budget-raises/a-lens-is-listed-once-*.json`): the pages face alone 617 / 260 bytes, every face 1,325 / 529, the embed with the studio handed in 1,312 / 529, and the guest host on the open kit 77 / 43, minified / gzipped. A hosted page's first load holds to its budget unraised.
- 774d39c: The map from altitude reads, and says where things stand (Roadmap f-map-readable, f-map-shows-state). `cityMap` lays the districts out by how the kinds relate: each kind one column past the furthest kind it points at, so the map reads upstream to downstream, left to right — People, Sessions, Questions, Findings, Opportunities, Actions — with the kinds of a column one above the other near what they point at, a cycle broken where the walk meets it, and a kind tied to nothing in a margin under the rest. Turned upright (`upright`), the same map reads top to bottom, which is how a window taller than it is wide draws it. A plot is two cells a side, or three past four records, so the biggest kind no longer takes the map; blocks are four cells apart (`BLOCK`, was five) and the largest side is three (`MAX_SIDE`, was four).
  
  On open the whole city fits the window — what is left of it beside a panel docked by the map — clear of the bar's corner controls and the zoom: the cell is the largest at which every plot and the name under it stand inside the room, and the names keep a fixed size on the screen whatever the zoom. Under each district is its name, its count and how many have a problem, on a solid backing — and, for a kind with a status, a bar of where its records stand ("9 open · 8 doing · 14 done", the words where a segment has room and always on hover). Where two names would collide the less pressing one says less (`LayoutNode.nameplate`: whole, short, over or beside its plot, or nothing until the camera comes closer); the most pressing is always said whole, over the edge of a neighbor's ground if it must, and a chosen record's link labels are placed clear of every name. Counts are said in the app's locale (`numberAsRead`). The lens names left the district: they are in the bar, and come up under the name when the district is hovered or focused. The marks nobody could explain are gone or said: the ◆ after a count and the open chevron's dot are gone from the map, "1 of 17 tied" is not said there, "moved ×" says "Reset view", the altitude control says "Close the map" (its name says where it lands), and the Key opens with what each mark on a block means. "From above", beside the zoom, sees the same ground from straight overhead (`tilt`, `TOP_DOWN`), kept in the browser.
  
  A district's blocks stand in rows by its kind's main choice field — its lifecycle's, else one called status, stage, state or phase — one row per value in declared order (`districtState`, `districtBlocks`): done lies flat and pale, waiting stands gray, active stands darker and taller, and a row cut for room says `+n` where it begins, never under the name. Color carries state, not kind: plots and blocks are the ground's own ink, and the strong colors are a problem or a record past its due date, lit; one somebody else changed since you last left the page, aglow; your own, rimmed. Coming down, the blocks say their records' names. The ground of the city — its plots, blocks, roads and their rules — is fetched the first time the scene rises (`plots.tsx`), with the bar of states and the Key's legend, so a page that never rises carries none of it.
  
  The layout no longer stands a billboard on a plot for a focused kind with a named picture — that picture opens in the panel beside the map — so a focused kind's district keeps all its ground. `@graview/core/city` is a new entry: the map alone (`cityMap`, `districtState`, `toIso`, `sideFor`, `BLOCK`, `MAX_SIDE`, `TILTED`, `TOP_DOWN` and the rest of what lays a city out), which the scene lays out with before it draws; `@graview/core/scene` keeps everything it had and adds `districtState`, `districtBlocks`, `TIER_HEIGHT`, `TILTED` and `TOP_DOWN`. `toIso` takes a tilt. A fitted thumbnail's district fills its whole block.
  
  Compatibility: the map moves. Every kind not hand-laid stands on a new plot, sides cap at three and blocks are four cells apart, so a thumbnail of an app (`sceneThumbnail`, `sceneDistricts`) draws a different picture and two plots declared five cells apart now leave a cell more street between them; a declared `plot` is still used verbatim. `@graview/core/city` is additive. `placeCity` takes the canvas and its insets, no longer a card base. `LayoutOptions.screenHeight` and `SCREEN_LEASH_CELLS` are removed with the billboard.
- dedefed: A near word finds the record, and says it is near (the Roadmap's f-semantic-search). A typo or another form of a word found nothing, and "the florist" did not find Bloom & Co, tagged flowers. Now, when the words find fewer than five records exactly, `search` (`@graview/core/search`) adds the records a near word finds, ranked after every exact hit: one slip in a word of 4–7 letters or two in a longer one ("tulpis" finds Tulips Galore, "flowr" the start of "flowers"), or another form of the word — a plural, -ing, -ed ("planting" finds "planted"). No model is asked for that, and it is fetched with Find as the matcher is: a near pass over 8,000–12,000 records adds a few milliseconds to a keystroke (6–16 ms in all on the gauntlet's and the discography's records). A host may give a ranker for what no spelling finds — `HostAi.near`, or `rank` on `search` — handed the words and only the records the seat may see, answering scores (0–1) and the record's word that was near; Find shows the exact hits at once and the ranker's when they arrive. Find's row for a near hit reads "Near “florist” — tags: flowers", the count says how many are near, and `search_graph` answers `near: true` with `why.matched`, so an agent knows it was not what it asked for. Going to a record by name and the conversation's "things called …" stay exact.
  
  Compatibility: a search's answer may now include near hits — a node hit's optional `near: true`, a `why` with `strength: "near"` and optional `asked`, `matched` and `by` (`"spelling"`, `"stem"`, `"meaning"`), and `SearchResult.later` when a ranker was asked; `search` takes `near` (false for exact hits only) and `rank`. `search_graph`'s name and input schema are unchanged; its description says what a near hit is, so a seat's tool-surface hash moves, and its answer may hold hits with `near: true`. `HostAi` gains `near`, `createToolRuntime` and `createMcpHttpHandler` take `near`, and `@graview/core` exports the types `NearBy`, `NearCandidate`, `NearScore` and `SearchRanker`. A ranker is handed only records the seat sees, past the kinds searched, the horizon and the conditions, so a hidden record's text never reaches a host's model (docs/stability.md). Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. The hosted page's first load grows by 121 B (70 B gzipped), the provider waiting for a ranker's answer, within its budget; the pages face, every face and the embed with the studio handed in are raised by the near matcher's measured size (`scripts/budget-raises/a-near-word-finds-the-record-*.json`).
- 11deba2: A host's ranker's word reaches an agent as the host's, and Find asks the ranker only once the typing pauses. The record's word a ranker says a near hit matched ("flowers") was handed to an agent as it came, at any length, as though the app had said it; a ranker that is a model can be steered by what it reads, so `search_graph` now marks it as words somebody else said — `why.matched` is `{ untrusted: true, authoredBy: "the host's ranker", text }`, the shape record text another seat wrote already takes — and a hit keeps at most 80 characters of it (`MATCHED_MOST` on `@graview/core/search`). Find searched on every keystroke and asked the host each time, so "florist" typed a letter at a time asked a model seven times for words nobody would see the answer to; it now waits 300 ms after a keystroke, and an ask a later keystroke overtook answers nothing without reaching the host.
  
  Compatibility: `search_graph`'s name and input schema are unchanged; on a near hit found by a host's ranker (`why.by: "meaning"`), `why.matched` is now an untrusted-text object rather than a string, and on every surface it is cut to 80 characters. Hits found by spelling or stem are unchanged. Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. docs/stability.md says it beside near hits.
- a4cfcdc: A chosen record's link line ends on the far record, or on its district, and nowhere else. A task opened on the map shows the list it is on as a chip inside its own card; the line took that chip for the far end and ran from the card to itself, bowing out of the card's lower edge to end on empty ground. The chips inside a chosen record's own card are no longer a far end, so the line reaches the list's building (or, if the list is not drawn on its own, runs dashed into its district with a count). `verify-lines` now asks it of a task chosen from altitude: every record-link line's end lies on a building or a district's ground outside the chosen card.
  
  On the ground the same rule holds. A record chosen draws its own links and nothing else: a connector that does not touch it is not drawn, rather than drawn dimmed, and each tie it draws says its relation at its far end in its kind's words, with a count where the far end is a district standing in for records the ground does not draw one by one ("what it is about: 2 topics"). Nothing chosen draws no tie. `verify-lines` asks both on the Program app's timetable, light, dark and on a phone: no record-level line with nothing chosen, and with one chosen as many lines as links said, none twice, none ending on nothing, none left once it is put down. The every-face budget is raised by 1_020 / 315 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/a-record-link-ends-on-the-far-record-every-face.json).
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 3b06199: A record can link another app, and the host says what that app is called (FR-171). A relation's `to` names kinds inside one document, so a scenario in one app could not point at a house in another. The first step is a field: `{ "type": "app-link" }` in a document, `appLinkField()` in a declaration, holding another app's address — `@owner/app`, an id its host knows it by, or an absolute http(s) address, at most 500 characters (`isAppAddress`). The store refuses anything else as `invalid`, `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` take the type, a migration keeps a value that is an address and clears one that is not, a spreadsheet's cell is read as one or refused in words (FR-177), `graview types` writes it as a string said to be another app's address, a form asks for it with "@owner/app or https://…" as its placeholder, and an act's tool schema gives it the address's `pattern` and `"x-graview-app-link": true` so an agent sets an address rather than a name. A host hands `resolveApp(address, viewer)` to `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the routed face's context, answering `{ name, icon?, headline?, href }`, `{ denied: true }` or `null`: the field draws as that app's icon, name and home headline, linked to `href`, on a record's page and in the scene's record panel, and as its icon and name on a list's row and a document card's field block. A viewer the host refuses sees "linked app (no access)" and nothing of the app. Without a resolver, or where the host knows no such app, the address is drawn as it is kept, a link only when it is an absolute http(s) address. The host is asked once per address and viewer, however many records link it, and a throw is taken as `null`. Where an act may write the field, a "Change" control beside the link edits the address. What draws it is a chunk fetched with the first app-link drawn: a hosted page's first load is 509 bytes larger (557 327 bytes over the vendors document, 556 818 on main), 501 handed a compiled app, and the scene before it draws 1 647, all under their budgets; the embed's pages face carries 1 884 / 1 607 bytes more minified / gzipped, every face 7 001 / 3 726, each raised by a file of its own. A relation whose `to` is another app's kind, an expression that walks it and the history of the far value are left for 0.2. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-171.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field type `app-link`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses the type by path, and a page that predates it refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `resolveApp`; `ReadableField` gains `app`, `ScalarField` gains `app`, and a document card's resolved field block gains `app`. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing an `app-link` field, and pass `resolveApp` to have one drawn as the app rather than as its address.

- ee15ae3: A store hands itself to a watching harness, and the watch reads its words (the ninth walk, W-193 and the hosted page's budget). `@graview/core` worked out the declaration's names, what a seat may not see and who had signed what, and told them to a harness's watch — 2.4 KB that every reader of a hosted page downloaded for a harness's sake, and the room the ninth walk's fixes needed under the hosted page's budget, which is not raised. - 0b722e4: Days, times and numbers are said in the app's locale, en-US first (FR-178). The eighth walk read "10 Sep 2023" in an app written for the United States: every day was said day first, in an order fixed in code, and no face knew a locale. Now every day, time and figure a person reads is said through `Intl` — a record's facts and its glance, a template's `{due}`, `{due | date}` and a moment's `{at}`, a block's field and figure, a rule's line and a rule's message, a creating act's line, an arrangement's headings ("Week of Aug 24, 2026"), the calendar's titles and labels, a declared timeline's hours, and the seat's lines ("On Thursday, Sep 17: …") — in en-US unless the app says otherwise: "Sep 10, 2023", "3:30 PM", "1,250". A moment (`2023-09-10T15:30`) is said with its time where it was said as it is kept. The app says its locale where it says its currency, `brand.locale` ("en-GB" keeps the day first, "de-DE" writes "1.250"), and a host that knows its reader passes theirs as `locale` on the embed (both faces), `GraviewProvider` and a pages `PageContext`, which wins over the app's. `describeApp` says what the locale makes of a day, a time and a figure; `editDocument`'s `set-brand` and `diffDocuments` say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for de-DE." The framework's own words stay English. `@graview/core` exports `dayAsRead`, `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead`, `numberAsRead`, `weekdayAsRead`, `dayOfMonthAsRead`, `monthAsRead`, `weekdayName`, `minutesAsRead`, `whenAsRead`, `localeFor`, `sayIn` and `DEFAULT_LOCALE`; each takes an optional locale. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-178.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool schemas are unchanged; `brand.locale` is the key it was, and now says days, times and numbers as well as money. The words a person reads change: a day is said "Aug 28, 2026" where it was said "28 Aug 2026", a moment with its time where it was said as stored, a number in a block or a rule's line with the locale's separators, a week's heading with its day in words, and the seat's day "Thursday, Sep 17" where it was "Thursday 17 Sep". `editDocument`'s sentence for `set-brand`'s `locale` and `diffDocuments`' for a changed locale say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for …"; a changed currency is its own sentence. What a host does: pass the reader's locale as `locale` on `mount`, and match words a test reads in en-US.
- 4c545b7: The city from altitude is laid out along the ground's own axes, and a relation's ribbon runs on the ground: a straight street beside each row of districts, and at most a bend or two between rows.
  
  The map of #263 stood upstream to downstream left to right across the screen, which on the tilted ground is the lattice's diagonal: every row of districts lay across the grain of the ground, so no street could run along one, and a ribbon between two districts of a row had either to climb a large V past the row or zigzag through the gutters behind each district between. Now `cityMap` lays the chain upstream to downstream along one of the lattice's axes — up and to the right on the screen, as a street in an isometric city runs — each kind one place past the furthest kind it points at, the kinds of one place side by side along the other axis, and the kinds tied to nothing in a row of their own parallel to the rest. Districts stand a block and a cell apart along both axes. In a tall window the chain runs down and to the right, folded into bands, where that stands the city larger. The window is still fitted to the whole city, clear of the chrome and of a panel docked beside the map.
  
  A district's name stands under its own plot, whole or short, and nowhere else: over a plot or beside it, it read as the neighbor's. Where names under their districts would meet, the districts stand further apart along the same axes (`cityMap`'s `pitch`), rather than a name moving. Where no spacing says every name inside the window — a phone, whose names are as wide as its city — the camera starts closer, at the spacing and closeness that name them all with the smallest city, and the city runs past the window's edges, with the signs on those edges saying what is there; a district the person chose is kept in sight. On a phone the panel beside the map is a sheet from the foot, and the city is laid out above it rather than under it (`--graview-sheet`), so what was chosen can still be pressed and asked for its acts; a lens's sheet stands at three fifths of the picture.
  
  A ribbon is routed on the lattice (`routeOnTheGround`): along the ground's axes through the gutters, a cell clear of every curb, never across a district; out of the middle of one district's curb square on and into the other's square on; bending only where it must, so a row's ribbons run its street straight; round the districts' name cards, measured where they were drawn — or, where the cards leave no way at all or only a long way round, under them as a street runs under a sign; keeping to the streets by the districts rather than the open ground beyond them. A stretch another ribbon already runs costs less, so relations that can share a street do, and each keeps one lane along the whole of a street, its own width from the next. The ribbons lie under the districts and their buildings, and follow the tilt the city is seen at, from above as from the side. A district chosen that the app has not got lists nothing beside the map. The router rides in the ribbons' own chunk, fetched when the city is first drawn; the embed's every-face budget is raised by 4_783 / 2_256 bytes for it and the phone's closer camera (scripts/budget-raises/ribbons-run-on-the-ground-every-face.json), and a hosted page's first load is no larger.
  
  Compatibility: every district the declaration does not place by hand stands at a new address once (a declared `plot` stands where it always did), so `graview describe`'s map and a scene thumbnail draw the new layout. `cityMap` takes a `pitch` hint and `@graview/core/city` exports `PITCH`; `placeCity` takes `keep`, the kinds whose districts stay in sight. `routeRibbons`, `tileOf` and `crossesAny` give way to `routeOnTheGround` in `@graview/react`'s ribbon module; none was exported from a package entry.
- 2016c5f: A relation's ribbon from altitude takes the short way along the ground between its two districts. It was routed along the lattice's gutters, which in a city laid out left to right drew it as a V — up to the top of the window and back between two districts in one row, or across the whole empty ground between two far apart. Now it runs straight from the corner of one district that faces the other to the corner of the other that faces it when nothing stands between them; along the row's street, just in front of its districts, when others in the row stand between; along the column's street, beside its districts, in one column; and otherwise with one bend, where a column's street meets a row's. Ribbons that share a street lie side by side in it, each its own width apart, a bundle rather than lines drawn over each other, and every run is straight. Ribbons now follow the tilt the city is seen at, from above as from the side (`routeRibbons`, `tileOf`). The router rides in the ribbons' own chunk, fetched when the city is first drawn: the embed's every-face budget is raised by 1_640 / 661 bytes for it (scripts/budget-raises/ribbons-take-the-short-way-every-face.json), and nothing a page loads first is larger.
  
  A district's status bar no longer cuts a narrow segment to "1…": it says a segment's count and value where both fit, its count alone where only that fits, and nothing where not even that does, and its words are on each segment's title.
  
  Compatibility: the scene changes what it draws. `streetPoints`, `curbToCurb` and `ribbonLanes` leave `@graview/react`'s ribbon module for `routeRibbons`, `tileOf` and `crossesAny`; none was exported from a package entry.
- 1b41c2d: Tab passes over a district the floating panel covers, and brings the one it lands on into the part that is seen. - 8da8189: A hosted page's first load is 31 KB lighter, and a change that spends more of it has to say so (FR-179). Graview Cloud's shell measured 562 KB of its 570, the framework's hosted page 573.8 KB of 579.7, and every release had spent a little of it. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any fetched chunk uses it, so a name a barrel the page imports re-exports rode up front as soon as a face, a form or the seat used it. Those names left the barrels for entries of their own: the matcher (`search`, `actsOn`, `touchWeights`, `parseQuery`, `matchNode`, `fold`, `squeeze`, `strengthOf`, `fragmentOf`, `searchableFields`, `describeSearched`) is `@graview/core/search`, handed to the provider by the scene and the Find box that search (`matchWith`, from `@graview/react/provider`); a record read in words and an argument's shape (`readableFields`, `pageSections`, `isLongText`, `describeNode`, `tellApart`, `argShape`, `describeArg`, `argumentWords`, `failureWords`, `viaSaid`) are `@graview/core/words`; an act's answer to its seat, every place an app has and the order things are made in (`answerSeenBy`, `placesOf`, `placesFrom`, `beginning`) are `@graview/core/seat`; judging the arrangement (`arrangementFindings`) is `@graview/core/check`'s; a card's sizes and a line's estimated width (`rosterRows`, `MARQUEE_WIDTH`, `estimateWidth` and the rest) are `@graview/layout/sizes`; and an address's arrangement is read apart from what a kind can be arranged by. A frame guest's host — `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost`, `createGuestLimiter`, `readAcross`, `readTheme` and the protocol's constants — is `@graview/guest/host/frame`, and `guestView` fetches it as the view is first drawn, the frame's height held meanwhile, asked for again when it did not arrive. The studio's door, the seat's panel and the lenses were already fetched with the face that draws them; a test now holds each of them, the frame guest's host and the matcher off the first load.
  
  `pnpm verify hosted` measures a hosted page at 556 818 bytes up front (543.8 KB, 187.4 KB gzipped), where main measured 589 206 (575.4 KB, 199.9 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less, 12.5 KB gzipped. Handed a compiled app (FR-123) it is 515 126 bytes (503.1 KB, 173.1 KB gzipped), where main measured 547 516 (534.7 KB, 185.6 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less. Before a face draws, with what it fetches as it is drawn, the scene is 876.3 KB and the pages 806.2 KB, each smaller than before. The budgets fall to 554.0 KB (191.5 KB gzipped) and 513.5 KB handed a compiled app (177.5 KB gzipped), which keeps 10 KB of room for the five features landing beside this one (FR-171, FR-172, FR-173, FR-176, FR-178, about 8 KB together), and a base is never edited upward again: a change that spends more files a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/` naming `"a hosted page"` or `"a hosted page handed a compiled app"`, the bytes it adds and the changeset that ships it, and that changeset says the kilobytes it adds up front ("1.2 KB"), or the test and the pull request's changeset check fail. `pnpm verify hosted` says the gzipped weight beside the minified, and docs/hosted-page.md carries both. Fetched all together, every face's chunks are 4 063 / 3 665 bytes more for the chunks the moved files split into, the studio handed in 1 816 / 2 926, the pages face alone 411 more gzipped (2 637 fewer minified), the guest host before a worker 282 / 288 and registering a worker view 223 / 639, each raised by a file of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-179.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Breaking for an import: the names above are no longer exported from `@graview/core`, `@graview/layout/view` or `@graview/guest/host`, and are imported from the entries named (their types — `ReadableField`, `FieldSection`, `ArgShape`, `AppPlace`, `Beginning`, `Hit`, `SearchResult` and the rest — stay where they were). What Graview Cloud does: import `placesOf` from `@graview/core/seat`, and `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost` or `readTheme` from `@graview/guest/host/frame`, if it uses them; its shell's `guestView` import is unchanged and now carries the frame's host only when a guest view is drawn.
- 2ca4722: From altitude the map draws only the lines that explain what is chosen, and a picture pressed on a district opens beside the map at a size a person reads.
  
  At rest every relation between two districts is one ribbon (`Ribbons`), run along the gutters between the blocks so it crosses no third district, as wide as the links along it, and said in words when pointed at: "146 features → 14 areas, the part it lives in", from the declaring kind's own description. No record has a line of its own. A chosen district keeps only its own relations' ribbons. A chosen record keeps none and draws its own links instead (`RecordLinks`): solid to a record the map draws on its own (its building, or its chip), ending in a dot, and dashed into a district for the records in it the map does not draw one by one, or for a relation that reaches more than six of them; each is labeled at its far end in its kind's words ("the part it lives in: tools", "what this part does: 46 features"), passes under the districts' names, and everything the record does not reach recedes. A link made less than sure (FR-172) stays so in the line the chosen record draws: dotted at the strength the scene draws a provisional link in, its label ending "· unsure" with what it was based on on hover. The connectors and the ties no longer draw at altitude, except for a change that just happened. The Key says the three manners first, and the unsure one at every height, and each relation's swatch is its ribbon at its own width. The roads are gone; `roadBetween` with them, and `streetPoints` moved to the ribbons.
  
  What is chosen from altitude stands in a panel beside the map (`MapPanel`, from `@graview/primitives` and `@graview/primitives/scene`, drawn by the Shell and an embed's scene face): docked at the side on a desk — the city is laid out in the room left, and Up, the Key and the ask field keep clear of it — and a sheet from the foot on a phone. A showing pressed on a district's marquee opens there at full size, in the mode a lens draws a page in, with its name, its count, the kind's description and Open to its own place; the map recedes but for its district, and no line is drawn over it. The billboard that drew the lens at the city's scale on its plot is gone, and the camera no longer flies closer to it. A chosen record lists its links there, a heading per relation in its kind's words, each record a press that chooses it; a chosen district lists its relations with how many each joins, and every record in it; a right-click on a record there, or on one in the lens, opens its acts as it does on the map — the card of chips that floated over the districts ("1 of 14 tied", "close", "+10 more — double-click to go in") is no longer drawn where the panel is. A district's "open", and a double-click on a district with no picture of its own, choose the district where a face says the panel is drawn (`BesideTheMap`, from `@graview/react`); a scene drawn without it, such as the studio's, still opens a district in place. The panel, the ribbons and a record's links are each fetched when first drawn, so a hosted page's first load and its scene before it draws hold to their budgets unraised; the embed's budgets are raised for them (scripts/budget-raises/the-map-draws-only-what-explains-the-choice-*.json): every face by 22_727 / 10_165 bytes, the pages face by 372 / 166 for the district's card asking whether the panel lists it, and the embed with the studio by 609 / 947.
  
  Compatibility: the scene changes what it draws, not any surface a host holds it to. `@graview/react` gains `Ribbons`' and `RecordLinks`' helpers (`mapChoice`, `districtOf`, `ribbonsOf`, `ribbonSentence`, `ribbonWidth`, `RIBBON_WIDTH`, `pickedFrom`) and `BesideTheMap`; `@graview/primitives` gains `MapPanel` and `mapPanelShows`. A selector on `.graview-road`, `[data-graview-screen]` or `.graview-screen-grip` finds nothing now: a relation from altitude is `[data-graview-ribbon]`, a record's link `[data-graview-link]`, and the panel `[data-graview-map-panel]`. `whereIs("screen:<kind>")` answers the district's box, where those looking at the picture now gather.

## 0.1.26

### Patch Changes

- 26dbe52: A picture or a document is put on a record from the page, through the host's own storage (FR-163). A host hands `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the Shell `files: { upload(file: Blob, name: string): Promise<FileValue>; maxBytes: number; kinds: readonly string[] }` (`HostFiles`), and `PagesApp` takes it in its context; `upload` stores the bytes and answers the value the record keeps. A file field's editor is drawn on every face where the record's values are changed — the scene's record, the routed record page, the routed face's forms and the strip's ask — and a field that holds nothing yet is drawn there too when an act may write it. It takes files picked, a photo taken (`<input capture>`, offered where the pointer is a finger), files dropped and files pasted, several at once for a list. A camera's photograph, and a JPEG or WebP bigger than a page needs, is drawn again in the page no larger than 2,048 pixels before it is sent (`resample`, which `downscale` now uses too); a HEIC the browser can read is sent as a JPEG, one it cannot read is sent as it is where the host takes HEIC and refused in words where it does not. What the host takes is said before anything is sent ("or drop or paste them here · pictures and PDF files, up to 10 MB"), and what it refuses is said in the editor's words: "“big.png” is 14 MB; this host takes files up to 10 MB.", "“quote.pdf” is a PDF file; this host takes pictures.", or the message `upload` threw ("“porch.png” wasn’t kept: the disk is full"). What is kept becomes the ordinary act that sets the field, which the room records and undo takes back, and a list's files may be given a caption, moved earlier and removed. Without `files` the editor says "This host doesn’t take files, so none can be added here." and offers no way to add one.
  
  For development and the harnesses, `@graview/ship/dev` has a door that keeps files in memory and serves them back (`devFiles()`, a Vite plugin, and `devFilesHandler`; `/__graview/files`, same-origin only, never in a build), and `@graview/ship/browser` has `filesAt(url, { maxBytes, kinds })`, the `files` a page hands that sends to such a door, or to a host's route that answers the same. The todo example's tasks take attachments through them under `pnpm dev`. `scripts/verify-files.mjs` drives it on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged by the seam itself. New: `HostFiles` from `@graview/core`; `files` on `FrameOptions` (so `mount` and `<Embed>`), `GraviewProviderProps`, `ShellProps` and `PageContext`; `HostFilesContext` and `useHostFiles` from `@graview/react` and `/provider`; `filesAt` from `@graview/ship` and `/browser`; `devFiles`, `devFilesHandler`, `DEV_FILES_PATH`, `DEV_FILES_KINDS` and `DEV_FILES_MAX_BYTES` from `@graview/ship/dev`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-163. What Graview Cloud changes: it passes the `files` its route already answers to `mount`.
- 26dbe52: A record can hold a picture or a document (FR-162). A document's field may be `{"type": "image"}`, `{"type": "file"}`, or a `list` with `"of": "image"` or `"of": "file"`; a declaration says the same with `imageField()` and `fileField()` from `@graview/core` (`photo: imageField().optional()`, `papers: z.array(fileField())`), which `toDocument` writes as those types. The value is what the host stored, `FileValue`: `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }`, its `href` a path on the host under its base (`/graview/files/3f9a….jpg`, as Graview Cloud answers, or one relative to the page). A value whose `href` has a scheme of any sort (`javascript:`, `data:`, `https:`), names another host (`//…`), holds a backslash or climbs (`..`, `.`, `%2e`) is refused, by the store, a form, a seat's call and an import alike (`isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`): a record is written by acts, seats and imports as well as by an upload, and a `data:` picture would put the bytes in the log, where they do not belong.
  
  A file field is drawn wherever the record is drawn. On a card in the scene and a row of a list on the routed face, the record's first picture is drawn small; on its page and in the scene's record, its pictures are a gallery, each opening full size in a dialog that gives the keyboard back to the picture it opened from; a document is said by its name, its type and its size, a PDF shown inline and anything else a download. Every picture says what it is: its caption, else its name. A view's blocks gained `{ "image": "photos", "as": "thumb" | "gallery", "label"? }` for a card, a row, a page or a blocks lens; the check refuses one that names no image or file field (`view-image`) and an `as` it does not know (`view-format`). What draws them is fetched with the first file field drawn, never in the first paint.
  
  A file's value is a value like any other: set by an act and said in the history by its names ("Change Fix the roof: photos → porch.jpg, gutter.jpg"), undone, exported and remixed as any field, found by its name and its caption, and written by `graview types` as `FileValue` (or `readonly FileValue[]`). A change that leaves files no record holds says which: removing a record says "Remove Fix the roof and its 3 files", and a migration's plan lists them (`MigrationPlan.files`) and names them in its words. What becomes of the bytes is the host's to decide. An agent's tools describe a file argument as the object it is, and a seat may set one to a value the host gave.
  
  The hosted page carries none of it up front that it did not pay for: 587,604 bytes up front where main is 589,213, and 546,632 handed a compiled app where main is 546,912 (see the changeset that moved the forms and the field reading to entries of their own).
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `image` and `file`, `of: "image" | "file"` on a list, and the `image` block, within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates them refuses them by path. The compiled format carries such a field within `graview-compiled@1`; a page that predates it refuses the compiled app (`compiled-shape`) and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. Check finding codes: `view-image` is new, an error only about a block that could not be written before. Tool names are unchanged; the input schema of an `edit-<kind>` (or any act) whose kind has a file field describes it as an object. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. New: `FileValue`, `imageField`, `fileField`, `isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`, `filesOf`, `isImage`, `fileKindOf` from `@graview/core`; `fileFieldsOf`, `firstPicture` and `fileWords` from `@graview/core/blocks`; `ReadableField.files`; `ArgShape`'s `{ type: "file", image? }` and `FormField`'s `control: "file"`; `MigrationPlan.files`; `Files` from `@graview/primitives` and `/pages`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-162. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing until it writes a file field; then it reads `capabilities().shipped` for FR-162 first, and serves each `href` it answers to whoever may read the record.

## 0.1.25

### Patch Changes

- 21365f6: An app fills the phone after it turns (FR-165). The embed took the height it was mounted with once, the whole-page Shell was `100vh`, the pages' seat stood in a fixed `100dvh`, and the scene passed over a box reported as nothing; on a phone turned to landscape and back, the app was drawn in the top half of the screen and stayed so, on both faces. `mount(element, { height: "viewport" })` now measures the screen itself: `innerHeight`, read again on `resize`, `orientationchange` and the visual viewport's `resize`, and once more a beat later for the phones that report a turn late. A height in viewport units — `100dvh`, `calc(100svh - 48px)`, the fallback inside a `var()` — is measured the same way, each `vh`, `dvh`, `svh` and `lvh` read as a hundredth of that figure. The Shell's height and the pages' seat follow the same figure (`useViewportHeight`, from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`), and the scene reads its box again on every `resize`, so a box that comes back from nothing is followed. The on-screen keyboard does not shrink the app: the figure is the layout viewport's, which the keyboard and a pinch leave alone, so a lens is not laid out again for half a screen while somebody types; the browser scrolls the field into view. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-165.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. `mount`'s `height` takes `"viewport"`, and a viewport unit in it is now measured rather than left to the browser.

## 0.1.24

### Patch Changes

- 9fe145e: A host's turn knows the app the seat is mounted with, so `describe_place` describes a lens place. Inside `HostAi.turn` the seat built its read tools from the store alone, and the chat's own runtime did the same, so a model asking `describe_place` for a lens or the declared home was told "is not a place this app has": the app never reached the seat. `GraviewProvider` now takes `app` and holds it on its context (`useGraview().app`), inheriting the app of a provider it is drawn inside; `mount` and `<Embed>` pass theirs on both faces, and `PagesApp`'s `context.app` reaches the provider under the pages. The chat hands it to its own tool runtime, to the workbench's agent seat and, as `ChatContext.app`, to a host's turn, whose `describe_place` now answers exactly as the runtime `graview mcp` serves does, for the seat that asked. With `app` given, a provider's reader `settings` default to `app.settings`. `graview create` writes `app` on the provider and on the pages' context. The hosted page carries 132 bytes fewer up front (589 625; 589 757 before), and as many fewer handed a compiled app (547 324; 547 456): the embed now hands the provider each option as given, which the provider's props accept as `undefined`, rather than spreading each one in only when it is set.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `app` on `GraviewProviderProps`, `GraviewContextValue`, `PageContext` and `ChatContext`. Changed: `GraviewProviderProps`' `principal`, `seats`, `people`, `memory`, `presenceTtlMs`, `presence`, `brand`, `onKeepLens` and `ai` also accept `undefined`, which means the same as leaving them out. What a host does: one that mounts its own `GraviewProvider` passes `app={yourApp}` (and `app` in `PagesApp`'s context) so its seat and its turn can describe the app's lenses and home; `mount` and `<Embed>` need nothing.
- a6c8da2: A problem opens on its repairs. Pressing a problem under the scene's standing selected what it named and nothing more — the scene's acts are the menu at the pointer, so at altitude, where the record is a cube in a shut district, its repair was on no surface at all, in every example. The press now opens the acts menu under the standing, on the problem's subject, its repairs first. `PointerMenu.on` may be given as `undefined`. (W-179, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- 129f2fa: The scene is said in named pieces. `Scene` was one function of 108 callees; what it does is now a hook or a part each named for what it is — the layout's options for the box it has (`useLayoutOptions`, in `scene-options.ts`), the hand's zoom, the billboard's height, the camera, the world laid out at rest and then moved, the steering hand, the first picture as a cut, the scene lending `whereIs`, the pointer reported only while somebody listens, the renderer handed the frame, what just happened resolved onto what is drawn, a card's gestures, the billboard's title bar (`ScreenGrip`) and the zoom in the ground's corner (`ZoomControls`). Nothing a person or a harness sees moves: the same elements, attributes and test ids, in the same order, and the scene face fetched before it draws is 29 bytes smaller (898 008 where it was 898 037).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and tool names and input schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same to the byte (589 757, and 547 456 handed a compiled app).

## 0.1.21

### Patch Changes

- eaffce3: The places list renders once as it opens (FR-158), and who else is here is on the bar, on both faces and a phone (FR-155). Graview Cloud, after 0.1.20: the bar ranks its places and folds the rest into More, and the row is weighed again whenever its room may change — a face arriving, a count on the standing, a brand's face loading — so a list already open could be drawn again with a new ranking, entries moving between the row and the list under a press already on its way; Playwright said "element is not attached", and on a slow phone a tap did nothing or hit the entry that moved there. While the list is open, the places now stay as they stood when it opened, every entry the element it was; the new ranking comes when it closes. `verify-chrome-quiet` holds an entry from before the open and presses it at once after, on Pages and on the scene, at a desk and on a phone, the scene also reached by the switch from Pages as Cloud reaches it.
  
  Presence was drawn as figures on the scene and nowhere else, so on Pages and on a phone a reader could not tell who was on the app with them, and Cloud stood a stack of its own under the bar. The one bar draws it now, wherever it is drawn — the Shell, the routed face's own bar, an embed's — from the presence the provider already holds: each other person a letter in a circle ringed in their own hue, as the person's own tool is drawn; an agent a letter in a square; three marks on a desk, two on a phone's bar, the rest counted, viewers not signed in among them. Pressed, it lists each by name — "Claude, for Ada" for an agent — and where each one is in the declaration's words (a picture by its title, a record by its label, a kind by its plural, "the whole thing" from above), never an id and nothing this reader may not see; on the scene a name goes where they are, as their figure does. It is a polite status to assistive technology ("Ada Lovelace and 2 viewers are here"), one mark per person however many tabs they have open, and nothing at all when nobody else is here, your own other tabs included. The marks and the list are fetched with the bar's panes once somebody else is here, so a reader alone carries none of it: the hosted page measures 593_425 bytes up front, 88 fewer than before, and the pages face alone 536_675, 118 fewer. `usePopover` takes `"here"` (`here-open`, `here-list`). `verify-who` holds it on the scene, on Pages and at a phone's width, and that the bar draws nobody once the others have gone. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-155 and FR-158.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, wire messages, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` names two more requirements. What Graview Cloud changes: once it takes this release it can delete `hereStack` in its shell (INTERIM(FR-155)) and its style, which the bar now draws in the bar's own row; and its views harness can press a place it held from before the list opened.
- 25019d4: The overview's address alone is the overview (FR-154). Graview Cloud: the framework wrote the overview at altitude as `/places/overview#overview=1` and read the altitude from the fragment alone. A fragment never reaches a server, so the same address back through any hop — the sign-in door, a stored link, a bookmark, or typed — came back as `/places/overview` and opened the overview descended on nothing: an empty scene whose control said "Up". Now `/places/overview` with no fragment, or with one that says nothing (`#`), opens the overview at altitude, on the Graview's face, under address routing in an embed and on a hosted page, and on any page that keeps its stop in the fragment (`UrlSync`, the whole-page Shell's `syncUrl`). The framework writes the overview at altitude over nothing in particular as the bare `/places/overview`, so the address it writes means the overview by itself: the bar's switch, Back to it, and "The whole thing" among the scene's places (`data-place-path="/places/overview"`). A fragment that says where it stands is read as it says: `#focus=t1` is the scene descended on that record, and `#overview=1&focus=…` the overview over it; a link written before, `/places/overview#overview=1`, still opens the overview and is tidied to the bare address in place, with no step Back would have to undo. Arriving at an address that says something no longer writes `#` and then pushes the stop: the page adopts the address with no entry. New in `@graview/layout/view`: `overviewStop` (the stop the overview's address holds) and `overviewFragment` (the fragment it carries for a view, none at altitude over nothing). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-154.
  
  A host can delete its interim: Graview Cloud's `restoreOverview` in `packages/client/src/shell.ts`, which put `#overview=1` back on a bare `/places/overview` before the framework read the window, does nothing the framework does not do now.
  
  The hosted page measures 120 bytes more up front (593 498, under its 579.7 KB) and 120 more handed a compiled app (547 895, under its 535.2 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-154. Changed: the overview at altitude is written as `/places/overview` with no fragment where it was `/places/overview#overview=1` (an embed's address bar, the scene's "The whole thing" `data-place-path`); a bare `/places/overview`, or `/places/overview#`, opens on the Graview's face at altitude, whatever face the host names, where it opened the scene descended on nothing. `where()` still reports the overview's stop as `#overview=1`.
- f062004: What the review after 0.1.20 found, mended. A reader on Pages is said where they are on the others' bars (FR-155): the routed face said nothing of its own, so a reader on Pages stood at the scene's last stop on everybody else's bar, or nowhere, and inside an embed the face's own provider and the embed's both said where one tab was, two places by turns. Now the routed face says its page as the stop that page is on the scene — a record, its kind's group, a picture, the overview, nothing for the home, Find or the problems — through the one provider that says it (`sayWhere` on `useGraview()`), so the bar says "On Pay the deposit" for a reader on that task's page, by its label and never its id, and their figure on the scene stands at it. In the list of who is here a kind is said by its plural as the declaration says it, never its name as written, and the status a screen reader hears is written once it is on the page, so the first person to come in is said.
  
  Pages has Activity (FR-152). Once its ten seconds were up the notice that offered a change back went, and a person on Pages with a mouse had no way to the change at all: only ⌘Z, or Activity on the scene. The routed face's bar now draws the scene's Activity — each change with its own way back — fetched with the bar's panes once anything has happened, so a face nobody has changed carries none of it (`BarActivity` on `@graview/primitives/pages`). A popover closed by Escape gives the keyboard to its trigger as it is in the document now, by its `aria-controls`, and again a frame later if it was left on `<body>`, which WebKit did after the bar's Activity. A notice on Pages says a new offer after a change was taken back, where it went on saying "Took back …". On the scene, pressing an offer's Take back left the keyboard on the notice's ×, so every offer after, said in the same place, was held by a keyboard nobody had put there and never went; the keyboard lands on the app's name now, as it lands on the page's title on Pages.
  
  A seat's first act says a broken rule's values (FR-159): the words were fetched by a problems call alone, so an act answered before one said `introduces` and `resolves` without them; the seat's tools now fetch them before any answer, and a preview and an undo answer without the parts a page draws a line with, as an act does. A seat that may not see the records a rule's line reads is told the line over what it sees: a line worked out over the whole graph could name up to three of them, or sum their values, in a problem the seat may see, on Pages and through the seat alike (FR-55); an act's answer to such a seat carries no line. A problem judged before the words arrived is the same problem after (`problemsMoved`, `shapedRules`), so the scene's activity no longer marked every broken rule new once they came. A long text in a line is said as its first 60 characters. A problem row's tooltip is the rule's own sentence only where it wrote one. `role-may-do-nothing` reads an empty `sees` as keeping nothing from anybody, as the store does.
  
  The overview's address tidies a bare `#` too (FR-154): `/places/overview#` opened at altitude and kept its `#`. Under address routing a bare `/places/overview` is the overview at altitude whatever stop the host hands, the address being the source of truth there. The hosted page measures 593_607 bytes up front, 28 more than before, under its 593_612 budget; the pages face alone 535_766, 1_704 more, Activity and who is where among it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: a presence's `stop` from a tab on Pages is that page's stop on the scene, where it was the scene's last; `violationKey(violation, shaped?)` takes the set `shapedRules(rules)` returns and keys a shaped rule's problem by its rule and record with or without a line; a seat served less than everything reads a problem's line, and its message's values, over what it sees, and no line in an act's answer; `preview_mutation` and `undo_batch` answer without `line`; a rule line says at most 60 characters of a text value; `POPOVERS.activity.drawn` names the embed and Pages too. New: `sayWhere` on the provider's context; `problemsMoved` and `shapedRules` on `@graview/core`; `BarActivity` on `@graview/primitives` and its `/pages`. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing; on Pages a person reaches what changed and takes a change back from the bar, and a reader there is said where they are.
